\underline{e}=(e_{1},\cdots,e_{m})
and
\underline{e}^{\ast}=(e_{1}^{\ast},\cdots,e_{m}^{\ast})
.

•

m_{q}=(k_{1},\cdots,k_{m})
, for
k_{i}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\textrm{ if }i\in\mathcal{S}^{q}_{0},M_{q}^{i}=u_{i},\\
-1&\textrm{ if }i=j\textrm{ or }k,(j,k)\in\mathcal{S}^{q}_{1},\\
0&\textrm{ if }i\in\mathcal{S}^{q}_{0},M_{q}^{i}=1_{2m}\textrm{ or }u_{i}^{-}.\end{array}\right.

•

\epsilon_{q}=(a_{1},\cdots,a_{m})
, for
a_{i}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\textrm{ if }i\in\mathcal{S}^{q}_{0},M_{q}^{i}=u_{i}^{-},\\
-1&\textrm{ if }i=j\textrm{ or }k,(j,k)\in\mathcal{S}^{q}_{1},\\
0&\textrm{ if }i\in\mathcal{S}^{q}_{0},M_{q}^{i}=1_{2m},\textrm{ or }u_{i}.\end{array}\right.

•

m_{X^{\ast}}(q)=m_{X^{\ast}}(M_{q})=\big(\prod_{i\in\mathcal{S}^{q}_{0}}m_{X^{\ast}}(M_{q}^{i})\big)\cdot\big(\prod_{(j,k)\in\mathcal{S}^{q}_{1}}m_{X^{\ast}}(M_{q}^{(j,k)})\big)
.

For
\widetilde{M_{q}}=(M_{q},t_{M_{q}})\in\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}^{z_{0}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
, decompose it as:

\widetilde{M}_{q}=(\prod_{i\in\mathcal{S}^{q}_{0}}\widetilde{M}_{q}^{i})\cdot(\prod_{(j,k)\in\mathcal{S}^{q}_{1}}\widetilde{M}_{q}^{(j,k)}),

where
\widetilde{M}_{q}^{i}=(M_{q}^{i},t_{M_{q}^{i}})
and
\widetilde{M}_{q}^{(j,k)}=(M_{q}^{(j,k)},t_{M_{q}^{(j,k)}})
. Given the cocycle values between any two distinct elements of
M_{q}^{i},M_{q}^{(j,k)}
are all
1
, we have:

\displaystyle t_{M_{q}}=(\prod_{i\in\mathcal{S}^{q}_{0}}t_{M_{q}^{i}})\cdot(\prod_{(j,k)\in\mathcal{S}^{q}_{1}}t_{M_{q}^{(j,k)}}).

\displaystyle\theta_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}}\widetilde{g})

\displaystyle=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}}p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\widetilde{M_{q}}p_{g})A\Big)(0)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},z_{g})\pi_{L,\psi}(\widetilde{M_{q}})\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)(0)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},z_{g})\prod_{i\in\mathcal{S}^{q}_{0}}\prod_{(j,k)\in\mathcal{S}^{q}_{1}}\pi_{L,\psi}(\widetilde{M}^{i}_{q})\pi_{L,\psi}(\widetilde{M}_{q}^{(j,k)})\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)(0)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},z_{g})m_{X^{\ast}}(q)^{-1}t_{M_{q}}\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{1/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})A\Big)(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon_{q}\cdot\underline{e}^{T}+\tfrac{1}{2}m_{q}\cdot\underline{e}^{\ast T})

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},z_{g})m_{X^{\ast}}(q)^{-1}t_{M_{q}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{m}}(-1)^{m_{q}\cdot n^{T}}e^{i\pi(n+\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon_{q})z_{g}(n+\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon_{q})^{T}}.

\displaystyle\theta_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}}\widetilde{g})

\displaystyle=\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}}p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(\widetilde{M_{q}}p_{g})B\Big)(0)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},z_{g})\pi_{L,\psi}(\widetilde{M_{q}})\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{3/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})B\Big)(0)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},z_{g})\prod_{i=1}^{m}\pi_{L,\psi}(\widetilde{M}^{i}_{q})\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{3/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})B\Big)(0)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},z_{g})m_{X^{\ast}}(q)^{-1}t_{M_{q}}\theta_{L,X^{\ast}}\Big(J_{3/2}(p_{g},z_{0})\pi_{X^{\ast},\psi}(p_{g})B\Big)(\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon_{q}\cdot\underline{e}^{T}+\tfrac{1}{2}m_{q}\cdot\underline{e}^{\ast T})

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},z_{g})m_{X^{\ast}}(q)^{-1}t_{M_{q}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{m}}(-1)^{m_{q}\cdot n^{T}}(n+\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon_{q})^{T}e^{i\pi(n+\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon_{q})^{T}z_{g}(n+\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon_{q})^{T}}.

For
z\in\mathbb{H}_{m}
, we define:

\displaystyle\theta_{1/2}^{\widetilde{M_{q}}}(z)

\displaystyle=m_{X^{\ast}}(q)^{-1}t_{M_{q}}\sum_{n=(n_{1},\cdots,n_{m})\in\mathbb{Z}^{m}}(-1)^{m_{q}\cdot n^{T}}e^{i\pi(n+\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon_{q})z(n+\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon_{q})^{T}}.

\displaystyle\theta_{3/2}^{\widetilde{M_{q}}}(z)

\displaystyle=m_{X^{\ast}}(q)^{-1}t_{M_{q}}\sum_{n=(n_{1},\cdots,n_{m})\in\mathbb{Z}^{m}}(-1)^{m_{q}\cdot n^{T}}(n+\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon_{q})^{T}e^{i\pi(n+\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon_{q})z(n+\tfrac{1}{2}\epsilon_{q})^{T}}.

Then:

•

\theta_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}}z)=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},z)\theta_{1/2}^{\widetilde{M_{q}}}(z)
.

•

\theta_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}}z)=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},z)\theta_{3/2}^{\widetilde{M_{q}}}(z)
.

Let
\widetilde{r}=(r,t_{r})\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)
. Let us define:

\widetilde{\lambda}:\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)\longrightarrow T;\widetilde{r}\longmapsto t_{r}\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r).

Lemma 8.16
.

\widetilde{\lambda}
is a character of
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)
.

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{s}=(s,t_{s})\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)
. Then:

\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{r})\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{s})=t_{r}\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r)t_{s}\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(s);

\widetilde{r}\widetilde{s}=[rs,t_{r}t_{s}\beta_{z_{0}}(r^{h_{-1}},s^{h_{-1}})];

\displaystyle\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{r}\widetilde{s})

\displaystyle=t_{r}t_{s}\beta_{z_{0}}(r^{h_{-1}},s^{h_{-1}})\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(rs)

\displaystyle=t_{r}t_{s}\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,s)\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(rs)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{Lem.}\ref{widetildec}}}{{=}}t_{r}t_{s}\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r)\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(s).

∎

8.8.5.

Let
\widetilde{r}=(r,t_{r})\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)
. Let
z=z_{g}\in\mathbb{H}_{m}
. Write
\widetilde{M_{q}}\widetilde{r}=\widetilde{s}\widetilde{M_{p}}
, for some
\widetilde{M_{p}}\in\mathcal{M}_{2m}
,
\widetilde{s}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)
. Then:

\displaystyle\theta_{1/2}^{\widetilde{M_{q}}}(\widetilde{r}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{r}z)^{-1}\theta_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}}\widetilde{r}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{r}z)^{-1}\theta_{1/2}(\widetilde{s}\widetilde{M_{p}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{r}z)^{-1}J_{1/2}(\widetilde{s},\widetilde{M_{p}}(z))t_{s}\widetilde{\beta}(s)^{-1}\theta_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{p}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{r}z)^{-1}J_{1/2}(\widetilde{s},\widetilde{M_{p}}(z))J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{p}},z)t_{s}\widetilde{\beta}(s)^{-1}\theta_{1/2}^{M_{p}}(z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{r},z)\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{s})\theta_{1/2}^{\widetilde{M_{p}}}(z).

\displaystyle\theta_{3/2}^{\widetilde{M_{q}}}(\widetilde{r}z)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{r}z)^{-1}\theta_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}}\widetilde{r}z)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{r}z)^{-1}\theta_{3/2}(\widetilde{s}\widetilde{M_{p}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{r}z)^{-1}J_{3/2}(\widetilde{s},\widetilde{M_{p}}(z))t_{s}\widetilde{\beta}(s)^{-1}\theta_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{p}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{r}z)^{-1}J_{3/2}(\widetilde{s},\widetilde{M_{p}}(z))J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{p}},z)t_{s}\widetilde{\beta}(s)^{-1}\theta_{3/2}^{\widetilde{M_{p}}}(z)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{r},z)\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{s})\theta_{3/2}^{\widetilde{M_{p}}}(z).

8.8.6.

Let
\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{M_{p}}\in\mathcal{M}_{2m}
. Write
\widetilde{M_{q}}\widetilde{M_{p}}=\widetilde{s}\widetilde{M_{r}}
, for some
M_{r}\in\mathcal{M}_{2m}
,
\widetilde{s}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)
.

\displaystyle\theta_{1/2}^{\widetilde{M_{q}}}(\widetilde{M_{p}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{M_{p}}z)^{-1}\theta_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}}\widetilde{M_{p}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{M_{p}}z)^{-1}\theta_{1/2}(\widetilde{s}\widetilde{M_{r}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{M_{p}}z)^{-1}J_{1/2}(\widetilde{s},\widetilde{M_{r}}(z))t_{s}\widetilde{\beta}(s)^{-1}\theta_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{r}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{M_{p}}z)^{-1}J_{1/2}(\widetilde{s},\widetilde{M_{r}}(z))J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{r}},z)t_{s}\widetilde{\beta}(s)^{-1}\theta_{1/2}^{\widetilde{M_{r}}}(z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{p}},z)\lambda(\widetilde{s})\theta_{1/2}^{\widetilde{M_{r}}}(z).

\displaystyle\theta_{3/2}^{\widetilde{M_{q}}}(\widetilde{M_{p}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{M_{p}}z)^{-1}\theta_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}}\widetilde{M_{p}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{M_{p}}z)^{-1}\theta_{3/2}(\widetilde{s}\widetilde{M_{r}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{M_{p}}z)^{-1}J_{3/2}(\widetilde{s},\widetilde{M_{r}}(z))t_{s}\widetilde{\beta}(s)^{-1}\theta_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{r}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},\widetilde{M_{p}}z)^{-1}J_{3/2}(\widetilde{s},\widetilde{M_{r}}(z))J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{r}},z)t_{s}\widetilde{\beta}(s)^{-1}\theta_{3/2}^{\widetilde{M_{r}}}(z)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{M_{p}},z)\lambda(\widetilde{s})\theta_{3/2}^{\widetilde{M_{r}}}(z).

8.8.7.

Recall notations from Sections
2.4
–
2.6
. Let
M_{q_{1}},\cdots,M_{q_{n}}
be the sets of
\mathcal{M}_{2m}
corresponding to each element
q_{1},\cdots,q_{n}
in
Q_{0}^{-1}(0)
. Let
n=|Q_{0}^{-1}(0)|=2^{2m-1}+2^{m-1}
. For each
q_{i}
, we choose an element
\widetilde{M_{q_{i}}}\in\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}^{z_{0}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})
. Let us define:

\widetilde{\gamma}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)}^{\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}^{z_{0}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})}\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1},\widetilde{M}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)}^{\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}^{z_{0}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})}\mathbb{C}.

Then
\widetilde{M}
consists of the functions
f:\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}^{z_{0}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}
such that
f(\widetilde{r}\widetilde{g})=\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{r})^{-1}f(\widetilde{g})
, for
\widetilde{r}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)
and
\widetilde{g}\in\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}^{z_{0}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})
. Let
\widetilde{e}_{i}
denote the function of
\widetilde{M}
, supported on
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)\widetilde{M_{q_{i}}}
and
\widetilde{e}_{i}(\widetilde{M_{q_{i}}})=1
. Then
\widetilde{M}=\oplus_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{C}\widetilde{e}_{i}
.

•

(a)

\widetilde{M_{q_{i}}}\widetilde{r}=\widetilde{s}\widetilde{M_{q_{j}}}
, for some
\widetilde{s}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)
iff
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)\widetilde{M_{q_{i}}}\widetilde{r}=\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)\widetilde{M_{q_{j}}}
.

(b)

If the above condition holds, then
[\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{r})(\widetilde{e}_{j})](\widetilde{M_{q_{i}}})=\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{s})^{-1}
, and
\operatorname{supp}[\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{r})](\widetilde{e}_{j})=\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)\widetilde{M_{q_{i}}}
. So
[\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{r})](\widetilde{e}_{j})=\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{s})^{-1}\widetilde{e}_{i}
, and
[\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{r}^{-1})](\widetilde{e}_{i})=\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{s})\widetilde{e}_{j}
.

•

(c)

\widetilde{M_{q_{i}}}\widetilde{M_{q_{j}}}=\widetilde{s}\widetilde{M_{q_{r}}}
, for some
\widetilde{s}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)
iff
[\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)\widetilde{M_{q_{i}}}]\widetilde{M_{q_{j}}}=\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)\widetilde{M_{q_{r}}}
.

(d)

If the above condition holds, then
[\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{M_{q_{j}}})(\widetilde{e}_{r})](\widetilde{M_{q_{i}}})=\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{s})^{-1}
, and
\operatorname{supp}\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{M_{q_{j}}})(\widetilde{e}_{r})=\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)\widetilde{M_{q_{i}}}
. So
[\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{M_{q_{j}}})](\widetilde{e}_{r})=\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{s})^{-1}\widetilde{e}_{i}
, and
[\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{M_{q_{j}}}^{-1})](\widetilde{e}_{i})=\widetilde{\lambda}(\widetilde{s})\widetilde{e}_{r}
.

•

Under the basis
\{\widetilde{e}_{1},\cdots,\widetilde{e}_{n}\}
, we identity
\widetilde{M}
with
M_{1n}(\mathbb{C})
, and write

\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{g})(\widetilde{e}_{1},\cdots,\widetilde{e}_{n})=(\widetilde{e}_{1},\cdots,\widetilde{e}_{n})M(\widetilde{g})
, for some
n\times n
-matrix
M(\widetilde{g})
. Then

\begin{array}[]{rcll}\widetilde{\gamma}:&\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}^{z_{0}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})&\longrightarrow&M_{n}(\mathbb{C});\\
&\widetilde{g}&\longmapsto&M(\widetilde{g}),\end{array}

gives a matrix representation.

We now define:

\Theta_{1/2}:\mathbb{H}_{m}\longrightarrow M_{1n}(\mathbb{C});z\longmapsto(\theta_{1/2}^{\widetilde{M_{q_{1}}}}(z),\cdots,\theta_{1/2}^{\widetilde{M_{q_{n}}}}(z))

\Theta_{3/2}:\mathbb{H}_{m}\longrightarrow M_{mn}(\mathbb{C});z\longmapsto(\theta_{3/2}^{\widetilde{M_{q_{1}}}}(z),\cdots,\theta_{3/2}^{\widetilde{M_{q_{n}}}}(z))

Theorem 8.17
.

Let
\widetilde{r}=(r,t_{r})\in\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}^{z_{0}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})
with
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}
. Then:

•

\Theta_{1/2}(\widetilde{r}z)=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{r},z)\Theta_{1/2}(z)\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{r}^{-1})=\pm\sqrt{\det(cz+d)}\Theta_{1/2}(z)\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{r}^{-1})
.

•

\Theta_{3/2}(\widetilde{r}z)=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{r},z)\Theta_{3/2}(z)\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{r}^{-1})=\pm\sqrt{\det(cz+d)}(cz+d)\Theta_{3/2}(z)\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{r}^{-1})
.

Proof.

1a) If
\widetilde{r}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)
, then by the results of Section
8.8.5
and the above (a)(b), the equality holds.

1b) If
\widetilde{r}=\widetilde{M_{q}}
, by the results of Section
8.8.6
and the above (c)(d), the equality holds.

1c) If
\widetilde{r}=\widetilde{s}\widetilde{M_{q}}
, for some
\widetilde{s}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)
, then

\displaystyle\Theta_{1/2}(\widetilde{r}z)

\displaystyle=\Theta_{1/2}(\widetilde{s}\widetilde{M_{q}}z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{s},\widetilde{M_{q}}z)\Theta_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}}z)\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{s}^{-1})

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{s},\widetilde{M_{q}}z)J_{1/2}(\widetilde{M_{q}},z)\Theta_{1/2}(z)\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{M_{q}}^{-1})\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{s}^{-1})

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{r},z)\Theta_{1/2}(z)\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{r}^{-1}).

The proof of Part (2) is similar.
∎

8.9.
The finite-dimensional representation I

8.9.1.

Let’s recall the notation for
\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
from Section
4.1
and for
\operatorname{Mp}^{z_{0}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
from Section
8.2
. By Lemma
7.3
, these two groups have the same associated cocycles but differ only in their centers. Therefore,
\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
is a subgroup of
\operatorname{Mp}^{z_{0}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
. Note that
\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}^{z_{0}}(\mathbb{R})
is another subgroup of
\operatorname{Mp}^{z_{0}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
. It consists of elements
(g,t)
with
t=\pm m_{X^{\ast}}(g)
, and
g\in\operatorname{Sp}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
. Since
\pm m_{X^{\ast}}(g)\in\mu_{8}
, we have:

\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}^{z_{0}}(\mathbb{R})\subseteq\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})\subseteq\operatorname{Mp}^{z_{0}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R}).

For
\widetilde{g}\in\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
, let us write
\widetilde{g}=\widetilde{g}^{z_{0}}\epsilon
, for some
\widetilde{g}^{z_{0}}=(g,t_{g})\in\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}^{z_{0}}(\mathbb{R})
and
\epsilon\in\mu_{8}
. We now define the canonical action of
\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
on
\mathbb{H}_{m}
via
\operatorname{Sp}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
.
Define:

•

J_{1/2}(\widetilde{g},z)=\epsilon^{-1}J_{1/2}(\widetilde{g}^{z_{0}},z)=\epsilon^{-1}t_{g}^{-1}\cdot\epsilon(g;z,z_{0})\cdot|\det J(g,z)|^{1/2}
.

•

J_{3/2}(\widetilde{g},z)=\epsilon^{-1}J_{3/2}(\widetilde{g}^{z_{0}},z)=\epsilon^{-1}J_{1/2}(\widetilde{g}^{z_{0}},z)J(g,z)
.

For
\widetilde{g}_{1},\widetilde{g}_{2}\in\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
, we have:

(1)

J_{1/2}(\widetilde{g}_{1}\widetilde{g}_{2},z)=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{g}_{1},g_{2}(z))J_{1/2}(\widetilde{g}_{2},z)
.

(2)

J_{3/2}(\widetilde{g}_{1}\widetilde{g}_{2},z)=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{g}_{1},g_{2}(z))J_{3/2}(\widetilde{g}_{2},z)
.

Let
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{m}(1,2)
and
\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})
denote the inverse images of
\Gamma_{m}(1,2)
and
\operatorname{Sp}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})
in
\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
, respectively.
We extend
\widetilde{\lambda}
to
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{m}(1,2)
by defining

\widetilde{\lambda}:\widetilde{\Gamma}_{m}(1,2)\longrightarrow T;\quad\widetilde{r}=(r,t_{r}\epsilon)\longmapsto t_{r}\epsilon\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r).

We also extend the above
\widetilde{\gamma}
to
\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})
by defining

\widetilde{\gamma}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{m}(1,2)}^{\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})}\widetilde{\lambda}^{-1},\quad\widetilde{M}=\operatorname{Ind}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{m}(1,2)}^{\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})}\mathbb{C}.

For
\widetilde{r}=(r,t)\in\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})
, where
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}
and
t\in\mu_{8}
, the following hold:

•

\Theta_{1/2}(\widetilde{r}z)=J_{1/2}(\widetilde{r},z)\Theta_{1/2}(z)\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{r}^{-1})
,

•

\Theta_{3/2}(\widetilde{r}z)=J_{3/2}(\widetilde{r},z)\Theta_{3/2}(z)\widetilde{\gamma}(\widetilde{r}^{-1})
.

We now reduce
\widetilde{\gamma}
to a representation of a finite group.

8.9.2.
m\geq 2

For
g\in\operatorname{Sp}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})
, write
g=rM_{q}
, for unique
r\in\Gamma_{m}(1,2)
and
M_{q}\in\mathcal{M}_{2m}
. Define the function:

f:\operatorname{Sp}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})\longmapsto\mu_{8};\quad g\longmapsto\widetilde{\beta}(r)\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,M_{q}).

We modify the cocycle
\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}
by
f
to obtain a new cocycle:

\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})f(g_{1})f(g_{2})f(g_{1}g_{2})^{-1}.

Lemma 8.18
.

Let
r,r_{1},r_{2}\in\Gamma_{m}(1,2)
and
g\in\operatorname{Sp}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})
. Then
\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r,g)=1=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2})
.

Proof.

1) By (
6.4
),
\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2})=1
.

2) If
g=M_{q}
, then

\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r,g)=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,M_{q})f(r)f(M_{q})f(rM_{q})^{-1}=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,M_{q})\widetilde{\beta}(r)1\widetilde{\beta}(r)^{-1}\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r,M_{q})^{-1}=1.

3) For general
g
, write
g=r_{g}M_{q}
. Then:

\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r,g)=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r,r_{g}M_{q})=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r,r_{g}M_{q})\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{g},M_{q})=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r,r_{g})\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(rr_{g},M_{q})=1.

∎

Lemma 8.19
.

\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,-)
defines a character of
\Gamma_{m}(2)
. In particular,
\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r)=1
, for any
r\in\Gamma_{m}(4,8)
.

Proof.

Let
g_{1},g_{2},g\in\operatorname{Sp}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})
and
r,r_{1},r_{2}\in\Gamma_{m}(2)
.

\displaystyle\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(rg_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r,g_{1})\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(rg_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r,g_{1}g_{2})\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2}).

\displaystyle\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r_{1}r_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r_{1}r_{2})\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r_{1})\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(gr_{1},r_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r_{1})\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(gr_{1}g^{-1}g,r_{2})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r_{1})\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r_{2}).

By Prop.
2.14
,
[\Gamma_{m}(2),\Gamma_{m}(2)]=\Gamma_{m}(4,8)
. So the last statement holds.
∎

Lemma 8.20
.

\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r)=-1
for some
g\in\operatorname{Sp}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})
and
r\in\Gamma_{m}(4)
.

Proof.

By Prop.
2.14
, the images of
u_{i}(4)
,
u_{i}^{-}(4)
generate
\Gamma_{m}(4)/\Gamma_{m}(4,8)
. Let us consider
r=u_{i}^{-}(4)=\iota_{i}(u_{1}^{-}(4))
and
g=M_{q}=\iota_{i}(u_{1})
, for
u_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}
,
u_{1}^{-}(4)=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-4&1\end{pmatrix}
. Then:

\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-4&1\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}-3&1\\
-4&1\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}-3&4\\
-4&5\end{pmatrix}\cdot\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix};

f(M_{q})=1,\quad\quad f(r)=\widetilde{\beta}(r)=\widetilde{\beta}(u_{1}^{-}(4))=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}},

\displaystyle f(M_{q}r)

\displaystyle=f(\iota_{i}(\begin{pmatrix}-3&1\\
-4&1\end{pmatrix}))=f(\iota_{i}(\begin{pmatrix}-3&4\\
-4&5\end{pmatrix})\cdot\iota_{i}(\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}))

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\beta}(\iota_{i}(\begin{pmatrix}-3&4\\
-4&5\end{pmatrix}))\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(\iota_{i}(\begin{pmatrix}-3&4\\
-4&5\end{pmatrix}),\iota_{i}(\begin{pmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}))

\displaystyle=\widetilde{\beta}(\iota_{i}(\begin{pmatrix}-3&4\\
-4&5\end{pmatrix}))=\widetilde{\beta}(\begin{pmatrix}-3&4\\
-4&5\end{pmatrix})=\big(\tfrac{-2}{5}\big)e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}=-e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{4}}.

\displaystyle\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}(g,r)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{c}_{X^{\ast}}(M_{q},r)f(M_{q})f(r)f(M_{q}r)^{-1}

\displaystyle=f(M_{q})f(r)f(M_{q}r)^{-1}

\displaystyle=-1.

∎

\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}
is indeed a cocycle on
\tfrac{\operatorname{Sp}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})}{\Gamma_{m}(4,8)}
. Let
\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}^{\prime}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})
,
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{m}(1,2)
,
\widetilde{\big(\tfrac{\operatorname{Sp}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})}{\Gamma_{m}(4,8)}\big)}
,
\widetilde{\big(\tfrac{\Gamma_{m}(1,2)}{\Gamma_{m}(4,8)}\big)}
be the corresponding covering groups associated to
\widetilde{c}^{\prime}_{X^{\ast}}
. Note that there exists an isomorphism:

\widetilde{\kappa}:\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}^{\prime}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})\longrightarrow\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z});[g,t]\longmapsto[g,f(g)t].

Lemma 8.21
.

(a)

\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\widetilde{\kappa}([r,t])=t
, for
[r,t]\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{m}(1,2)
.

(b)

\widetilde{\gamma}\circ\widetilde{\kappa}\simeq\operatorname{Ind}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{m}(1,2)}^{\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}^{\prime}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})}[\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\widetilde{\kappa}]^{-1}\simeq\operatorname{Ind}_{\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{m}(1,2)}^{\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}^{\prime}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})}[1_{\Gamma_{m}(1,2)}\cdot\operatorname{Id}_{\mu_{8}}]^{-1}\simeq\operatorname{Ind}^{\widetilde{\big(\tfrac{\operatorname{Sp}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})}{\Gamma_{m}(4,8)}\big)}}_{\widetilde{\big(\tfrac{\Gamma_{m}(1,2)}{\Gamma_{m}(4,8)}\big)}}[\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\widetilde{\kappa}]^{-1}
.

Proof.

1)
\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\widetilde{\kappa}([r,t])=\widetilde{\lambda}([r,f(r)t])=\widetilde{\lambda}([r,\widetilde{\beta}(r)t])=t
.

2) It is a consequence of part (1).
∎

8.10.
The finite-dimensional representation II

Recall the isomorphism:
\overline{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\to}}\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}^{z_{0}}(\mathbb{R})
from Lemma
8.8
. Let
\overline{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})
,
\overline{\Gamma}_{m}(1,2)
and
\overline{\Gamma}_{m}(2)
denote the inverse images of

\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}^{z_{0}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})
,
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{z_{0}}_{m}(1,2)
and
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{m}(2)
in
\overline{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
, respectively. Let
\overline{\lambda}=\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\iota
. Recall the notation
\lambda
from (
8.1
). Then:

\widetilde{\kappa}^{-1}\circ\iota:\overline{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\to}}\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}^{z_{0}}(\mathbb{Z})\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\widetilde{\kappa}^{-1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}^{\prime}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z}).

(\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\widetilde{\kappa})\circ(\widetilde{\kappa}^{-1}\circ\iota)=\widetilde{\lambda}\circ\iota=\overline{\lambda}.

Lemma 8.22
.

\overline{\lambda}(r,\epsilon)=\lambda(r)\epsilon
, for
(r,\epsilon)\in\overline{\Gamma}_{m}(1,2)
.

Proof.

\overline{\lambda}(r,\epsilon)=m_{X^{\ast}}(r)\epsilon\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r)=\epsilon\lambda(r)
.
∎

Lemma 8.23
.

\overline{\lambda}(\overline{r})\in\mu_{2}
, for
\overline{r}=(r,t)\in\overline{\Gamma}_{m}(2)
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.22
,
\overline{\lambda}(\overline{r})=\lambda(r)t
. According to Lemma
8.12
, for
r_{1},r_{2}\in\Gamma_{m}(2)
, we have:

\lambda(r_{1}r_{2})=\lambda(r_{1})\lambda(r_{2})\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(r_{1},r_{2}).

Hence, it suffices to show the result holds for the generators
u_{ij}(2)
,
u^{-}_{ij}(2)
,
v_{ij}(2)
of
\Gamma_{m}(2)
.

1)

If
r=u_{ij}(2)
or
v_{ij}(2)
, by Example
6.4
,
\widetilde{\beta}(r)=1
. In this case,
m_{X^{\ast}}(r)=1
.

2)

If
r=u^{-}_{ij}(2)
, with
i\neq j
, then by Example
6.6
,
\widetilde{\beta}(r)=1
. In this case,
j(r)=2
,
x(r)=-4
,

m_{X^{\ast}}(r)=\gamma(x(r),\psi^{\tfrac{1}{2}})^{-1}\gamma(\psi^{\tfrac{1}{2}})^{-j(r)}=\gamma(-1,\psi^{\tfrac{1}{2}})^{-1}\gamma(\psi^{\tfrac{1}{2}})^{-2}=e^{\tfrac{\pi i}{2}}e^{-\tfrac{\pi i}{2}}=1.

3)

If
r=u^{-}_{ii}(2)
, then
r=\iota_{i}(u^{-}(2))
, for
u^{-}(2)=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
-2&1\end{pmatrix}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
. Hence

m_{X^{\ast}}(r)\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(r)=m_{X^{\ast}}(u^{-}(2))\widetilde{\beta}^{-1}(u^{-}(2))=e^{\tfrac{i\pi}{4}[\operatorname{sgn}(2)]}e^{\tfrac{i\pi}{4}[-\operatorname{sgn}(2)]}=1.

∎

Definition 8.24
.

\overline{\gamma}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle Def.}}{{=}}\operatorname{Ind}_{\overline{\Gamma}_{m}(1,2)}^{\overline{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})}\overline{\lambda}^{-1}\simeq\widetilde{\gamma}\circ\iota
.

For
\overline{g}=(g,\epsilon)\in\overline{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
,
\iota(\overline{g})=[g,m_{X^{\ast}}(g)\epsilon]\in\widetilde{\operatorname{Sp}}^{z_{0}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
. Let us define the canonical action of
\overline{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
on
\mathbb{H}_{m}
via
\operatorname{Sp}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
.
Define:

•

J_{1/2}(\overline{g},z)=\epsilon^{-1}\cdot\epsilon(g;z,z_{0})\cdot|\det J(g,z)|^{1/2}
.

•

J_{3/2}(\overline{g},z)=J_{1/2}(\overline{g},z)J(g,z)
.

Lemma 8.25
.

For
\overline{g}_{1},\overline{g}_{2}\in\overline{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{R})
, we have:

(1)

J_{1/2}(\overline{g}_{1}\overline{g}_{2},z)=J_{1/2}(\overline{g}_{1},g_{2}(z))J_{1/2}(\overline{g}_{2},z)m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1}g_{2})m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1})^{-1}m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2})^{-1}
.

(2)

J_{3/2}(\overline{g}_{1}\overline{g}_{2},z)=J_{3/2}(\overline{g}_{1},g_{2}(z))J_{3/2}(\overline{g}_{2},z)m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1}g_{2})m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1})^{-1}m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2})^{-1}
.

Proof.

\overline{g}_{1}\overline{g}_{2}=(g_{1},\epsilon_{1})(g_{2},\epsilon_{2})=(g_{1}g_{2},\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}\overline{c}_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1},g_{2}))=\overline{(g_{1}g_{2})}
.

1)

\displaystyle J_{1/2}(\overline{g}_{1}\overline{g}_{2},z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\iota(\overline{(g_{1}g_{2})}),z)m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1}g_{2})

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\iota(\overline{g_{1}})\iota(\overline{g_{2}}),z)m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1}g_{2})

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\iota(\overline{g}_{1}),g_{2}(z))J_{1/2}(\iota(\overline{g}_{2}),z)m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1}g_{2})

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{g}_{1},g_{2}(z))J_{1/2}(\overline{g}_{2},z)m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1}g_{2})m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{1})^{-1}m_{X^{\ast}}(g_{2})^{-1}.

2) It follows form part (1).
∎

Theorem 8.26
.

Let
\overline{r}=(r,\epsilon)\in\overline{\operatorname{Sp}}_{2m}(\mathbb{Z})
with
r=\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
c&d\end{pmatrix}
,
\epsilon\in\mu_{2}
. Then:

•

\Theta_{1/2}(\overline{r}z)=J_{1/2}(\overline{r},z)m_{X^{\ast}}(r)^{-1}\Theta_{1/2}(z)\overline{\gamma}(\overline{r}^{-1})=\epsilon\sqrt{\det(cz+d)}\Theta_{1/2}(z)\overline{\gamma}(\overline{r}^{-1})
.

•

\Theta_{3/2}(\overline{r}z)=J_{3/2}(\overline{r},z)m_{X^{\ast}}(r)^{-1}\Theta_{3/2}(z)\overline{\gamma}(\overline{r}^{-1})=\epsilon\sqrt{\det(cz+d)}(cz+d)\Theta_{3/2}(z)\overline{\gamma}(\overline{r}^{-1})
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\Theta_{1/2}(\overline{r}z)

\displaystyle=\Theta_{1/2}(\iota(\overline{r})z)

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\iota(\overline{r}),z)\Theta_{1/2}(z)\widetilde{\gamma}(\iota(\overline{r})^{-1})

\displaystyle=J_{1/2}(\overline{r},z)m_{X^{\ast}}(r)^{-1}\Theta_{1/2}(z)\overline{\gamma}(\overline{r})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{-1}J_{1/2}(r,z)m_{X^{\ast}}(r)^{-1}\Theta_{1/2}(z)\overline{\gamma}(\overline{r})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\epsilon\sqrt{\det(cz+d)}\Theta_{1/2}(z)\overline{\gamma}(\overline{r}^{-1}).

\displaystyle\Theta_{3/2}(\overline{r}z)

\displaystyle=\Theta_{3/2}(\iota(\overline{r})z)

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\iota(\overline{r}),z)\Theta_{3/2}(z)\widetilde{\gamma}(\iota(\overline{r})^{-1})

\displaystyle=J_{3/2}(\overline{r},z)m_{X^{\ast}}(r)^{-1}\Theta_{3/2}(z)\overline{\gamma}(\overline{r})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{-1}J_{3/2}(r,z)m_{X^{\ast}}(r)^{-1}\Theta_{3/2}(z)\overline{\gamma}(\overline{r})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\epsilon\sqrt{\det(cz+d)}(cz+d)\Theta_{3/2}(z)\overline{\gamma}(\overline{r}^{-1}).

∎
